# The Postpartum Suite: design system

The baseline every new or rebuilt page inherits. Written 5 October 2026 for the
award-site pipeline, extracted from `v1.css`, `care.css`, `HANDOVER.md` and the
Brand Pack. It describes what exists; it does not change anything.

**Order of authority:** `TPS Brand Pack.html` and `TPS Brand Fonts.pdf` (project
root) beat this file. This file beats Aura, 21st.dev and every reference site.
`TPS Design/TPS-DESIGN.md` is stale and is not a source.

Tags: **[TJ]** said by TJ, **[Brand Pack]** from the brand documents,
**[build]** how the live CSS does it today.

---

## 1. Colour [build, from `v1.css :root`]

| Role | Token | Value |
|---|---|---|
| Page ground | `--canvas` | `#F3EFEC` |
| Paper | `--paper` | `#FFFFFF` |
| Blush | `--blush` | `#E2CAC6` |
| Rose mist | `--rose-mist` | `#D7C2C9` |
| Ink | `--ink` | `#0A0A0A` |
| Ink soft | `--ink-soft` | `#2C2C2C` |
| Ink mute | `--ink-mute` | `#5A524F` |
| Coral mid | `--coral-mid` | `#C86D58` |
| Coral deep | `--coral-deep` | `#B95A4B` |
| Ember (action) | `--ember` | `#A64C45` |
| Ember deep (hover) | `--ember-deep` | `#8C362F` |
| Sand (rules) | `--sand` | `#C9B9B5` |
| Haze | `--haze` | `#E3D7D3` |
| Hairline | `--hairline` | `rgba(10,10,10,.08)` |
| Hairline firm | `--hairline-firm` | `rgba(10,10,10,.14)` |

Components point at role aliases (`--action`, `--emphasis`, `--rule`), never
at raw values. New pages load `v1.css` first for these tokens, as
`programme.html` already does.

Photography and film carry the warmer residential range recorded in the Agent
side `PROJECT.md` (ivory, oatmeal, caramel wood, cocoa). That range lives in
imagery, not in UI tokens.

## 2. Type [Brand Pack]

| Job | Face | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Display, headlines | Instrument Serif | Capitals on the homepage |
| Labels, interface | Montserrat 300 to 600 | |
| Anything read | Karla | Body, paragraphs, quotes |
| One accent line | Quentin | Usually one per page, large. Guidance, not a rule: TJ can ask for more (23 Oct 2026) |
| Badges, section numbers | IBM Plex Mono | Never prices or dates |

Never a numeral in a title ("Week One"). All caps headings sit at one cap
height. Josefin Slab, TPS Display, Forum and Corinthia are out.

## 3. Shape and depth [Brand Pack]

- Radius: badge 6px, button 8px, card 10px, media 16px.
- **No shadows anywhere.** Depth is hairlines, edges and overlap.
- **No dark sections.** A dark photograph is allowed.

## 4. Layout [build]

Content max 1080px, reading measure 62ch, gutter 24 / 28 / 48px by width,
section rhythm 96px (64px on phones), nav 80px tall.
Ease: `cubic-bezier(0.23, 1, 0.32, 1)`.

## 5. Signature effects, keep on every page [TJ, 5 Oct 2026]

### Frosted glass

TJ wants it maintained throughout the site. The live recipe:

```css
backdrop-filter: blur(14px) saturate(1.2);
-webkit-backdrop-filter: blur(14px) saturate(1.2);
```

Two fills, chosen by what sits behind:

- **Over photographs or film, carrying text** (`.tab`, `.deskcard` on Care):
  `background: rgba(243, 239, 236, .62)`. At 62% ink stays above AA whatever
  the picture does.
- **Over pale grounds, as a chip** (`.tv2__nav`, nav chips): `rgba(140, 127,
  121, .04)`, `.46` on a dark frame. [TJ] chose 0.04 from a rendered ladder.
  **Do not raise it to fix faintness. That is the effect.**

No border on glass. No shadow. The nav also has `.nav--glass` and a lighter
`.veil` at `blur(7px)`.

### Aurora background

The moving coral weather field, adapted from the 21st.dev Aurora Background.
Lives in `v1.css` (`.aurora`, `.aurora__field`), used in the confinement and
programmes acts of `v1.html` and on `programme.html`. TJ wants it kept.

Its recipe is not a taste setting; each value was measured:

- Two layers in brand coral, blush and rose mist over white windows. The moving
  layer runs at 123 degrees with different periods so it never reads as bars.
- `filter: blur(46px)` holds the gradient banding under the visible threshold.
- `opacity: .46` is the strongest it goes while every line of copy clears
  contrast. At .58 it failed.
- A linear mask fades it in and out so no section edge shows; an off centre
  elliptical mask gives the weather a direction.
- `animation: aurora 62s linear infinite`.

Reuse the existing classes. Do not re-import the 21st.dev original, which
bands, uses blues and violets, and relies on `background-attachment: fixed`
that breaks on iOS.

## 6. Locked [TJ]

Never changed by work on another page or section:

- `v1.html` is the homepage and the latest version of the site. Since
  5 Oct 2026 it opens with `hero`, then `bath`, then a straight cut into the
  seven chapter house film (`baby-handover` to `window-exit`), then
  `confinement`, `care`, `programmes`, `treatments`, `dissolve`, `close`.
  `care.html` now opens on `housekeeping-zones`.
- The hero, and the scroll from hero into bath, a fade. Moved back from
  `care.html` to the top of `v1.html` on 5 Oct 2026, exactly as it was [TJ].
  Guarded by `lab/herolock.mjs` and the Stop hook, which watch `v1.html` and
  passed against the saved record after the move.
- Any section TJ has called finished.
- The single Menu chip nav.
- **Components TJ wants kept throughout the build:** _to be listed by TJ._

## 7. Rules that bind every page [TJ]

- No dead scroll.
- Visible transitions between sections are fine and judged case by case.
- No em dashes or hyphens in any copy, titles or metadata.
- Questions are not build orders. Production files change only on "build".
- Never commit or push without asking.
- Never publish an hourly price. (The ban on a unit price was overruled by Ami, 23 Oct 2026: the Programmes page shows a per day price only. See the section at the end.)

## 8. Site header [TJ, 5 Oct 2026: every page has the same header]

The header is written once, in `site-header.html`. `sync-site-header.py`
stamps it into every page (v1, what-we-do, care, programmes, single-treatments,
about, specialist, programme). To change the header, edit `site-header.html`
and run `python sync-site-header.py`. `python sync-site-header.py --check`
reports any page that has drifted. Per page differences (the lockup link, the
Enquire link, whether the bar starts compact) live in the PAGES table at the
top of the script. A new page must be added to that table.

The menu's "What We Do" tab still points at care.html until the new page is
approved. The nav behaviour code still exists in three places (v1.js,
programme.js, inner-nav.js). The markup is unified; the behaviour is not yet.

## 9. Design language v2 (5 Oct 2026)

Study: maisonfav.aura.build, a single page architectural template by someone
else. It is a reference for moves, never a source of code. Tags: [TJ] decided by
TJ, [proposal] Claude's, awaiting TJ.

### Decisions
- [TJ] Headlines stay in CAPITALS, Instrument Serif, as the Brand Pack says.
- [TJ] Buttons stay Ember with soft rectangle corners. No black capsules, no
  black blocks. [TJ] chose the option that adds a small round arrow chip on the
  button. [proposal flag] The Brand Pack says an arrow glyph reads as shop
  urgency (for text links). Needs the client's sign off before it ships.
- [TJ] The Aurora background and the frosted glass stay as they are.
- [TJ] No custom mouse cursor, no dot that follows the pointer, anywhere.
- [TJ] One page at a time. Order: What We Do (strong copy exists), About (draft
  exists), Home (verbal plan, to be dictated). The other pages follow.

### What is taken from MaisonFav
1. The label with a short line before it (OUR PERSPECTIVE). Built as a hairline
   rule in CSS, never a typed dash. One per section at most.
2. The statement sentence: one large sentence that carries a section's idea.
   [proposal] Sentence case Instrument Serif, no slanted word unless TJ
   approves it. Headlines above it stay in capitals.
3. The photo stack: big rounded photographs that slide over one another as the
   page scrolls. New device for this site, to be built once and reused.
4. Cards that open wider on touch. Already owned (the treatments accordion).
5. A quiet figures row. Plex Mono figures only, real figures only.
6. The pace: wide spacing, few things on screen at once.

### What is not taken
Cool grey ground, black capsule buttons, black cards, sentence case headlines,
the cursor dot, its fonts, and every line of its code.

### Every page is built the same way
1. Hero. 2. One statement sentence. 3. The page's own sections from the device
list. 4. One signature moment invented for that page and not used elsewhere.
5. Questions if the page needs them. 6. The shared close and footer. The shared
header comes from site-header.html.

### Still to decide
- Whether statement sentences may carry slanted words.
- The arrow chip on buttons (client sign off).

## 10. Header band at the top of the homepage [TJ, 5 Oct 2026]

At the top of the page the tall header sits on a band of the page colour, not on
the photograph. The hero photograph starts below it and its top edge fades into
that colour, so there is no line. The photograph keeps its full width and leans
down (object-position 62% 68%) so the bottom of the picture is kept. The pinned
stage still spans the whole window, so the pin and the steam to bath handoff
are unchanged. Everything from the bath onwards was checked pixel for pixel and
is identical. The bar's frosted strip has no outline, so its edge appears only
as content passes under it. Variable: --hero-top in v1.css.
The hero only guard is retired. See LOCKED.md for how finished sections are
protected now.

## 11. One header, one fold, every page [TJ, 5 Oct 2026]

Every page opens with the tall header (three line wordmark, circle in the
corner) on a band of the page colour, and folds to the short bar with the
circle on scroll, exactly as the homepage does. The animation is pure CSS in
v1.css. The homepage folds after its hero handoff (v1.js). Every other page
folds after 56px of scroll (nav-fold.js, or the header's data-nav-fold value).
Each page's first content starts below the tall bar (--hero-top). The page
specific bar styling the programme builder and the booking page used to carry
is removed so the header really is the same everywhere. The booking page's
photograph now starts below the bar with the same faded top as the homepage.
Open: the booking page still shows its own logo lockup under the photograph, so
the brand now appears twice at the top of that page.

## 12. Housekeeping zones: bathroom removed [TJ, 5 Oct 2026]

The bathroom stop is gone from the zones section on What We Do: the film was
recut (nursery lit, half a second dissolve, whole house lit), the sentence
phrase, the screen reader line, the room button and the poster are removed, and
zones.js has no bathroom leg. Uncut originals: assets/video/zones.with-bathroom.mp4
and zones-m.with-bathroom.mp4. The section is UNLOCKED until TJ approves it,
then record the lock again (see LOCKED.md).

## 13. Titles carry no full stop (TJ, 5 Oct 2026)
No full stop at the end of any title or subtitle (h1, h2, h3, display lines). Exceptions are rare and deliberate. A full stop inside a title between two sentences ("Same care. Longer stay") is left alone. Body copy, ledes and captions keep their normal punctuation. Applied to every live page on 5 Oct 2026.

## 14. Housekeeping zones layout (5 Oct 2026)
No sentence under the rooms. The rooms are an accordion on a fine vertical line: the room in view gets the ember marker and a few small words (Karla) open beneath its name, one open at a time, driven by scroll and by tap. Wide screens: title left, house centred, accordion right, pinned by its top so it never jumps. Phone and tablet (up to 1099px): a sandwich, title above the house, accordion below; the house plays first and the words arrive after it. Description text is Lorem Ipsum until the client supplies it.

Zones film fit (5 Oct 2026): the film is a 1600 by 892 plan filling the stage below the header. In windows wider than that shape it is sized to its own shape, centred, and feathered at the edges (container query in care.css), so the whole house, both floors, is always in view. Checked at 1100 to 2560 wide, heights 600 to 1300.

Zones on phones and tablets (5 Oct 2026, TJ): tap driven, not scroll driven. Up to 1099px wide the section is an ordinary block: title, house, rooms as horizontal tabs on a fine line with a sliding ember marker, the open room's words appearing and expanding beneath. The house plays its arrival once when it comes into view and stops on the living room; a tap lights a room using stills cut from the same film (assets/img/zones-m-*.webp). The tab copy is built from the accordion text in care.html, so real copy goes in one place. Desktop keeps the scroll scrubbed film.

## 15. Site footer (5 Oct 2026, TJ)

One footer on every page, stamped from `site-footer.html` by `sync-site-footer.py`, styled by `site-footer.css`. Ground and motion are band 3 (Charcoal silk) from `Fuchsia-variants.html`, at the study's own strength and 62 second drift: Ink Mute ground, Blush, Rose Mist and Haze drifting over it. The text is Ink, not white, because the study's light glow cannot carry white text. A 22 percent Canvas veil lifts the darkest patches so Ink measures 4.7 to 1 or better at every point of the drift; lowering the veil fails contrast. The logo is the normal black line art.

## 16. The Promise moved to What We Do (5 Oct 2026, TJ)

"Do we offer night care?" (data-section `dissolve`, named The Promise) now lives on `care.html`, after A Day With Us and before Your Postpartum Specialist. It is gone from `v1.html`. Its motion is in `promise.js`, copied from `v1.js` (`actPeak`, `navTheme`); `v1.js` still contains the originals, which return at once when the section is absent.

The header's ink eases through the night frame instead of flipping (5 Oct 2026, TJ). `promise.js` `navTheme()` sets `--nd` (0 day, 1 night) from scroll, blending between 0.20 and 0.49 of the dissolve, and back out as the frame's lower edge sweeps the bar. The CSS is the `[data-nd]` block at the end of `what-we-do.css`; `v1.css` is untouched. `is-dark` is still set past halfway for the mobile open menu rules.

## 17. Tailored To You rebuilt (5 Oct 2026, TJ)

One frame, no scroll inside it: the section is the height of the window below the 80px header (`min-height: max(660px, 100svh - 80px)`, tighter under 780px tall), checked at 1366 by 650, 1280 by 720, 1440 by 900 and 1920 by 1080. Phones and tablets (900px and under) flow: the photograph bleeds off the top and sides and dissolves downward into the heading, then the rows, then the stay. Concepts A, B and C are kept in `lab/tailored-concepts/`; A, Rooms, is built.

Ground is band 5 (Silk on coral) of `Fuchsia-variants.html`, unchanged. TJ's decisions on 5 Oct 2026: Quentin is the heading "Tailored To You" in white (not the bold line under it); no vertical lines anywhere in the section; every visible word is the client's copy, so no "Your stay" label (the only additions are the sentence "In N days, your specialist will have given you:" and the three count labels, which TJ approved); the photograph bleeds off the right edge, top and bottom of the section and dissolves into the ground toward the text (a mask, so the silk shows through), and follows the open paragraph (care-exterior, care-living, hero_night-shift_v1).

Colour roles on the coral: white Quentin heading; Ink bold lead, row openers, paragraphs, ruler numerals and count labels; Canvas cream (`--tl-cream`) for the three counts. Ember and Ember Deep fail on this ground. The drift is calmed in the top and bottom bands where white and cream sit (`--tl-calm`, .16) and the Canvas veil is only in the middle band where the ink is (`--tl-veil`). Worst measured: white heading 3.95, cream counts 3.16 (large type, needs 3), ink 5.3.

The stay: a scale of 30 day ticks with 5, 7, 14 and 30 at their true positions, a dot at each stop, a round marker on the rule at the chosen length. On arrival the marker travels 0 to 5 over 1.4 s and the counts climb with it (default is 5 days). Click a numeral or the rule, press the arrow keys, or drag the marker, which settles on the nearest stop. Count up is the original, 650 ms. Notes in `research/care-tailored/`. Previous versions: `lab/*.before-*.bak`.

Update, later on 5 Oct 2026 (TJ): the bold line under the heading is Instrument Serif in capitals like the zones, with no final full stop (section 13). Every room starts closed on every screen; a tap opens one, a tap on the open one closes it, and the photograph stays on the last opened. Photographs: hero_night-shift_v1 (the specialist at the cot) is the first and the default, then care-living, then care-exterior. The photograph is 52vw wide and dissolves across its whole width with a long eased mask (about 100 percent of its width), starting past the text column; on phones the fade is longer. The progress bar is white: 2px base rule, 5px drawn line, solid white 20px marker with no border, white dots at the stops; the chosen length's number turns white and the others stay Ink; the labels under the three figures are white. To keep a white selected number legible the silk streak now sits behind the rows (full strength) and is held at `--tl-streak` (.3) behind the numbers; a thin Ember Deep tint (`--tl-sink`, .16) sits under the bottom band. Worst measured: selected number 3.16, figures 3.35, labels 4.44 and the sentence 4.38 (both just under 4.5), heading 4.0.

Update 3, 5 Oct 2026 (TJ): the part of the scale not yet reached is black (Ink); the part reached stays white. The rules between the rows and the plus signs are white, and the line above the first row is gone. "In N days, your specialist will have given you:" is white. The rows (opener and opened text) and the labels under the figures use the header's text: Montserrat 500, .02em tracking, mixed case, header size, not Instrument Serif, Karla or tracked capitals. The silk streak is full behind the rows only; it is calm and the Ember Deep tint (`--tl-sink`, .2) starts higher so the white numbers, sentence and labels sit on a darker ground. Worst measured: selected number 3.7, figures 4.0, labels 4.7, sentence 4.5, heading 3.8; ink 4.3 (opened row two opener) to 5.2.

Update 4, 5 Oct 2026 (TJ): the lengths that are not chosen are faded (ink at .34, .8 on hover); the rules between the rows are Ember Deep (`--tl-rule`), not white; the labels under the figures are in title case ("Freshly Cooked Meals") via `text-transform: capitalize`. Four photographs, one per state: all closed is hero_night-shift_v1, then care-kitchen, care-living and care-exterior for the three rows, so every open, switch or close changes the picture.

Footer update, 6 Oct 2026 (TJ): the logo is the stacked lockup (circle with the three line wordmark under it, `the-postpartum-suite-logo.webp`, turned white) filling the left third of the footer; the three columns are equal. The tagline "The care mothers deserve, at home" has no full stop, is set in Quentin in white, and runs the full width of the footer, centred above the closing line. The footer locks for `care.html` and `v1.html` are UNLOCKED until TJ approves; then run `node lab/lockcheck.mjs record care.html:site-footer "note"` and the same for `v1.html`.

Footer update 2, 6 Oct 2026 (TJ): the stacked logo is 9rem tall, centred vertically in the row and shorter than the Explore column beside it (144px against 230px at desktop, about 42px of air above and below). Explore and Contact are the higher level: .92rem, 600, capitals, white; the items under them are .84rem, 400. Footer locks stay UNLOCKED until TJ approves.

Footer update 3, 6 Oct 2026 (TJ): the stacked logo is 10.8rem tall (20 percent larger, 173px at desktop, still shorter than the 230px Explore column, 29px of air above and below). On desktop the logo, Explore and Contact are spread across the footer's width with equal gaps between their visible edges (278px each at 1440 wide), so the logo's left edge and Contact's right edge line up with the copyright and Privacy Policy text. Phones and tablets keep the stacked grid. Footer locks stay UNLOCKED until TJ approves.

Footer update 4, 6 Oct 2026 (TJ): on phones and tablets (760px and under) the logo is the inline lockup (circle with the one line wordmark beside it, `logo-inline.webp`, white) centred at the top, via a `<picture>` that keeps the stacked logo for desktop. Explore and Contact sit side by side under it; at 460px and under the first column takes its natural width and the email breaks after the @ (a `<wbr>`) rather than squeezing the list. The Quentin tagline never wraps: on phones it is sized from the window width (`calc((100vw - 3rem) / 15)`), and `white-space: nowrap` applies at every width. Footer locks stay UNLOCKED until TJ approves.

The logo file has clear space round the artwork, so it is pulled in with negative margins on the link (`.sf__brand a`). Phones are unchanged. Footer locks stay UNLOCKED until TJ approves.

Footer logo loads eagerly (the `loading="lazy"` was removed, 6 Oct 2026): both logo files are 5 to 12 KB, and lazy loading made it start fetching only as the footer scrolled into view, so it appeared after the text.

## 20. Tailored To You comparison (6 Oct 2026, proposal awaiting TJ)

A second Tailored To You section sits directly beneath the original in `care.html` for comparison. The original remains unchanged. The proposal now uses the original Tailored To You moving Silk on Coral background and the original contrast safe colour roles instead of the flat Coral Deep field.

The programme day selector and result sentence and figures now sit above the accordion rows, making the accordion the final part of the section. The proposal photograph remains 44 percent of the window on desktop, with a short fade over the first 36 percent of the photograph so most of the image remains crisp. The programme scale drops the thirty minor ticks, uses a thinner reached line and smaller result figures. On phones the photograph remains first and the copy flows beneath it, with body copy held at .88rem. Both accordions, photographs and stay scales operate independently. In the proposal, the progress bar has no ruler ticks, uses a 3px translucent white base and a 6px white reached line, and animates from zero to day five when the scale enters view. The proposal accordion uses one continuous frosted veil rather than separate cards: no divider lines or drawn border, and the existing glass background and blur continue fully to the left and right sides instead of dissolving through a side mask. All accordion text is white, and the white disclosure control is a plus when closed and a minus when open. The existing cream to blush focus gradient remains behind the open row. The homepage scrolling video panel glass treatment was tested and rejected by TJ because it looked worse; do not reapply it. The proposal lead No two recoveries are the same, so no two stays are either is set as two deliberate lines. Its type size scales from the proposal column so the visible first line, not merely its container, matches the full frosted accordion width edge to edge. Measured at 1440px: 649.91px text against a 650px panel. At 390px: 341.84px text against a 342px panel. Two copies of the current proposal sit beneath the original Tailored To You section so TJ can develop a separate idea without disturbing the current proposal. The newest copy, `tailored-proposal-2`, is the dynamic photograph palette experiment. Its default photograph remains `hero_night-shift_v1.webp`; the third accordion photograph is `mother-and-carer-on-sofa-with-baby-no-hijab.webp`, replacing the house exterior. Each state changes only `--tl-ground` over 900ms: closed clay `#885136`, kitchen taupe `#A37E62`, living terracotta `#956548`, sofa taupe `#A27E64`. Closing the open row returns to the closed clay base. In this newest copy only, the lead No two recoveries are the same, so no two stays are either is one continuous line sized to the full frosted-box width: 650.13px at 1440px and 341.98px against a 342px box at 390px. Its colour is Ink Soft `#2C2C2C`, verified directly in `TPS Brand Pack.html`. TJ rejected Ember Deep `#8C362F` for this line and found full Ink `#0A0A0A` too severe; Ink Soft is the approved trial between them. In this newest copy only, the summary “In N days, your specialist will have given you:” is more prominent at Montserrat 600 and `clamp(.95rem, .82rem + .35vw, 1.12rem)`; its changing day figure is also 600. A single visible “Days” label sits above the 5, 7, 14 and 30 values so the scale communicates its unit immediately; the radiogroup retains its accessible name, “Length of stay in days”. The smaller Days label sits at the far left of the scale, 16px above the progression line. It clears the moving marker throughout its opening animation and does not occupy a separate layout row. The number size, number spacing and progression bar geometry inherit the proposal directly above without overrides, matching exactly at 1440px and 390px. In this newest copy only, closed accordion questions and plus signs use Montserrat 300 for a slimmer state. The open question and revealed paragraph return to the existing Montserrat 500, and the minus uses the existing 400 weight. The existing Canvas, Haze, Blush and Rose Mist silk highlights, masks, opacity, 44px blur and 62 second motion remain identical to the proposal above; interactions remain independent. The accordion focus gradient is clipped to the glass box and driven by a ResizeObserver, so its top and full height continuously match the open row throughout expansion and it cannot sit below the copy or escape the box. In the newest copy only, the three accordion rows are replaced by one continuous passage with paragraph spacing. The opening portion is visible, Read more reveals the remaining copy, Read less closes it, and the existing highlight covers the full expanded panel. The closed state shows `hero_night-shift_v1.webp` on clay `#885136`; expansion changes to `care-kitchen.webp` and kitchen taupe `#A37E62`, and closing returns both to their original state. All passage and toggle text remains Montserrat 300 in both states. The earlier original and proposal accordions remain unchanged. This comparison is not approved or locked.

## 21. Multi page preservation process (TJ, 6 Oct 2026)

This prototype is one multi page website. Never classify a page as a disposable variant merely because another page is becoming the current focus. Before any rename, replacement or deletion, inventory the complete route list, identify each file as a live page, shared fragment, reference, experiment or backup, and show TJ the exact proposed file operations. Preserve the current files in `lab` before any approved destructive operation. Restore or replace only the named files. Afterward, verify every live page, shared header and footer synchronization, internal links and section locks. Never commit or push unless TJ asks.
## 22. Tailored For You consolidation (TJ, 6 Oct 2026)

The original `tailored` section and `tailored-proposal` comparison were removed from `care.html` at TJ's request. The former `tailored-proposal-2` is now the only Tailored section, using `id="tailored"`, `data-section="tailored-for-you"`, the section panel name “Tailored For You”, while the visible Quentin heading remains the client copy “Tailored To You”. When TJ identifies sections by the numbered dev panel, a rename request applies to `data-section-name`, not visible page copy, unless he explicitly asks to change the heading. Its dynamic photograph palette, day scale, lightweight unified Read more passage and kitchen expansion state remain intact. On phones, the photograph fades into the current dynamic ground and ends before the title begins, leaving 24px of clear separation at 390px. Its mobile frame is 250px tall at 390px and both retained photograph states use a 58 percent vertical focal position, trimming a little from the top to reduce the section height. Desktop image geometry remains unchanged. The three changing specialist result groups remain in one horizontal row rather than stacking vertically. Whichever day is selected, 5, 7, 14 or 30, its number becomes fully opaque white to match the thick completed portion of the progression line. The unified Tailored glass panel uses `var(--r-card)`, resolving to the same 10px radius as the homepage Nursery glass panel. ## 23. Tailored To You horizontal scroll choreography (TJ, 6 Oct 2026)

TJ approved a horizontal entrance and exit for the consolidated Tailored section after consultation with Scrollcraft, taste skill, Design DNA and 21st.dev. On desktop, the Tailored section first rises naturally from beneath the Specialist section. During that normal vertical entrance, the copy begins nearer the centre and moves left into its final position while the masked photograph glides in from the right. There is no desktop pin. Specialist and Tailored remain connected in normal document flow, divided only by their section boundary and colours. The horizontal entrance runs from the first pixel of Tailored reaching the viewport bottom until the complete 920px section sits beneath the 80px header. The heading leads the movement, followed separately by the day scale, result figures and glass panel. The photograph begins later, travels farther and settles more slowly through the existing fade, so the layers are triggered together without appearing anchored together. Desktop uses a 1.15 second scrub and mobile uses a lighter .85 second scrub. The middle composition settles with only a subtle photographic scale change, keeping the day selector and Read more passage interactive while the section continues moving vertically. On exit, the photograph returns right and fades while the copy moves back toward the centre before the FAQ enters. On mobile, the same directional sequence runs without pinning and over a shorter distance; normal vertical scrolling remains in control. Reduced motion receives no pin or added transforms. Expanding or closing Read more refreshes ScrollTrigger after the height transition so the exit remains aligned. The section is clipped horizontally to prevent transformed media from creating page overflow.
## 24. Tailored To You scroll controlled day sequence (TJ, 7 Oct 2026)

This decision supersedes the no desktop pin sentence in section 23. On screens wider than 900px, Tailored still enters through the approved differential horizontal choreography. During that natural entrance the existing scale and results travel from 0 to 5. Once the complete section reaches 80px below the fixed header, it holds for 1.5 viewport heights while the marker moves through 5, 7, 14 and 30. The marker uses eased travel between the real scale positions. The selected number and the three results change together at the nearest programme state. The section then releases normally, and its differential exit begins only after the hold finishes.

The existing controls and scroll are one system on desktop. Clicking a programme number, clicking the scale or settling a drag moves the page to that programme's scroll position instead of competing with the current scroll state. Opening Read more remains independent and preserves the active programme when ScrollTrigger refreshes after the panel height changes.

Screens 900px wide and under remain tap controlled with no pin and no scroll controlled programme changes. Reduced motion also has no pin and keeps direct programme selection. Verified at 1440 by 900 and 390 by 844 with no browser errors. The desktop section top remained at 79.98px through the full hold, click seeking landed with a zero pixel difference, mobile registered zero Tailored pins, and the expanded glass remained inside the section. Verification images are `lab/tailored-scroll-days-desktop.png` and `lab/tailored-scroll-days-mobile.png`. The prior JavaScript is preserved at `lab/what-we-do.before-tailored-scroll-days.js.bak`.

Fix: the exit timeline in `what-we-do.js` fades `.tail__field` to opacity 0 (from .7) over .16 of the timeline, so it has dissolved by about 25 percent of the exit scroll; the held look is unchanged (it only starts once the exit begins) and it returns when scrolled back. Measured in the clear left margin (columns 60, 160, 300): the strongest horizontal edge falls from 11 at exit 0 to 5.9 at 120px, 4.3 at 225px and 3.3 from 330px on (the page's own floor). The day title and the lighter band (TJ, 8 Oct 2026): the line is now "Care arranged around the hours you need most" (TJ's edit of the client's "need it most", no full stop), set as a title in Instrument Serif capitals, sized by promise.js to 96 percent of the day column's content width (28px at 1440, 25 at 1280, 38 at 1920; it wraps to two lines on phones), and pushed to the top of the day half on desktop (margin-bottom auto in the flex column), about 44px below the header. It arrives with the glass as the question fades (opacity from --dn-g .35 to .68), so the two never overlap. Between the title and the Day Care text the day glass is lighter: a second, vertical mask on the day half (full at 0 to 17 percent, .42 from 31 to 39 percent, full again by 53 percent), composited with the horizontal one, so more of the room shows through with no hard edge; desktop only. Title contrast measured at 7.4 to 8.6 to 1 across sizes. Backups `lab/*.before-dn-title.*`.

Frost without the snap (TJ, 8 Oct 2026): the glass became fully frosted in one step at the end of its phase. Cause, measured: the glass faded in through its container's opacity, and in Chrome a backdrop-filter inside a part transparent parent cannot see behind it, so only the tint faded and the real blur switched on at full opacity (background sharpness held at 18.7 at g .99 and fell to 0.6 at g 1). Fix: the container is never faded; the blur radius and the tint alpha scale with the scroll instead (`blur(calc(18px * var(--dn-b)))`, `rgba(..., calc(.66 * var(--dn-g)))`). The blur follows --dn-b = g to the power 2.4, because blur is perceived on a curve (the first pixel removes far more detail than the last), so a straight ramp front loads the change. Measured at a coarse scale the background now softens across the middle of the phase with no step at the end. Sequence `lab/daynight-frost-sequence.png`. Backup `lab/day-night.before-frost-fix.css.bak`. The photograph's drift (scale to 1.07) was already continuous.

Who is it for, copy with the treatments (TJ, 8 Oct 2026), superseding the copy below: the earlier version removed the burden but left out the other half of the offer, that she is also pampered at home every day (massage, hot stones, belly binding). Accuracy note for the client: "every day, a treatment" is true to her copy (massage several times a week, binding daily, other treatments between). Who is it for, copy (TJ, 8 Oct 2026), replacing the earlier answer text (superseded): written to hit the reader's pain, not describe the service. She is expecting, possibly only just, with a life that is already full, and the baby is a whole new life that needs everything; the service takes the load so she can heal; the close honours what she has done without overreaching. Four blocks, ours not hers, to be presented to the client by TJ: "If you are expecting, and wondering how a life that is already full will hold a baby too." / "The nights. The meals. The washing. A home that still needs running while your body is asking to rest." / "For those first weeks, one trusted person takes all of it. You heal, you feed, you sleep. Everything else is done." / "Nine months of carrying. You have earned this." 48 words. Decisions: the opening names the avatar ("If you are expecting") and carries the anxiety about a full life holding a baby, with no timing, because readers range from just pregnant to nearly due; "manage it all" was dropped as vague; alternatives offered and not used: a softer close "Let someone carry you", a darker "quietly dreading the weeks after". Text-wrap balance on the blocks so no word is stranded on its own line; at most three lines per block at 1920, 1440, 1366, 1280, 1100 and 390. Backup `lab/care.before-who-copy.html.bak`.

## Day and Night: night falls fully before the glass, one white change (TJ, 8 Oct 2026)
Night was easing across the whole pin, so it was only 40 percent dark when the glass began. Now it reaches full night at the start of the glass (promise.js, ease p / GLASS_FROM), and the beat after the clock is longer (DN_BEAT_VH 0.25 to 0.7) so full night has scroll of its own. Measured: night opacity 0.56, 0.71, 0.86, 1.00 at 30, 38, 46, 54 percent of the pin; glass starts after 1.00.
The header ink and the small title and paragraph now share one curve, ndAt() with ND_FROM 0.66 and ND_TO 1.11 (scroll vh over the clock pin), so they turn white together, while the photograph passes from about 50 to 85 percent night. Measured header --nd against text colour at six scroll points: identical. The older separate text crossover is removed.

## Day and Night: deeper night, longer hold (TJ, 8 Oct 2026)
Night is full at 0.2 vh after the clock (NIGHT_FULL), and the beat before the glass is 1.3 vh (DN_BEAT_VH), so about a screen of full night is held before the glass starts (measured: night 1.00 from 40 percent of the pin, glass first moves after 55). The night photograph is also darkened as it completes (brightness down to .66, saturation .92 via --dn-nv) because the asset itself leaves the sofa fairly light. The white change window moved to ND_FROM .55, ND_TO .95 to suit; header and text stay on the one curve.

## A Day With Us: vertical timeline (TJ, 8 Oct 2026)
Adapted from the 21st.dev Product Timeline (hyperiux, id 26930; notes in research/what-we-do/refs/21st-product-timeline-hyperiux.md), turned vertical. New files: day-timeline.css, day-timeline.js; new section in care.html (id day-with-us, data-vt) placed before the original.
Behaviour: a sticky stage (640vh section, 760vh on phones). One scrubbed timeline: the axis line draws down while the track slides up so the tip stays at 56 percent of the screen (60 on phones); each hour, as the tip reaches it, grows a stem and a dot, then its time (Plex Mono), title (Instrument Serif) and text (Karla) rise out of masked lines (own line splitter, no SplitText). Entry windows are 12 percent of the scroll and overlap by half. Hours alternate left and right of a central axis; on phones the axis runs down the left edge. Hours far above the tip dim softly to 28 percent. The title sits on the one frosted glass recipe where the component has its image: the "The day" label, "A Day With Us" and the lead line, the client's copy. The closing line is the last stop. All 11 hours, full text (2.30pm in full).
Ground: the footer's Aurora construction in the light FAQ palette (coral #C86D58, Rose Mist, Blush) at 40 percent over Canvas with a 16 percent Canvas veil, drifting over 62 seconds. Ember Deep for the line and dots, Ink for times and titles, Ink Soft for text.
Reduced motion: .is-static, the finished list, no sticky. Rebuilds on width change only. Scroll trigger refreshPriority -13 so it measures after the pinned sections above.
The original list section stays directly below as the backup (id day-with-us-original, data-section a-day-with-us-original), unchanged and still animated by what-we-do.js. Backups: lab/care.before-vertical-timeline.html.bak, lab/what-we-do.before-vertical-timeline.css.bak (the CSS file itself was not changed).
Measured at 1440x900 and 390x844: sticky holds, no horizontal scroll, no page errors, the long 2.30pm entry clears its neighbours.

## A Day With Us timeline: card entrance, hours compressed (TJ, 8 Oct 2026)
Card entrance: as the section scrolls into view (section top from 88 to 12 percent of the screen, before it holds) the glass lifts 28px and frosts in (--vt-ci), the label fades (--vt-cl), and the title and lead rise out of masked lines, staggered. Hours: the pitch is clamp(92px, 11.5vh, 124px), down from clamp(200px, 30vh, 330px). day-timeline.js reads the rendered pitch (a clamp() in a custom property comes back unresolved from getComputedStyle) and gives each hour more height only where its own text would run into the next hour on the same side (two hours on) or into the close, with a 22px gap (--vt-pitch-gap). Measured at 1920x1080, 1440x900, 1280x720, 1100x800: pitch 124/104/92/92, minimum same side gap 22px, close 31px under the last hour, no errors. Phones: hours auto height, bottom padding clamp(22px, 4vh, 34px).

## A Day With Us timeline: card label removed (TJ, 8 Oct 2026)
The small "The day" label and its rule are removed from the glass card of the new timeline. The card is now the title and the lead line only. The original backup section keeps its label.

## A Day With Us timeline: the line starts at the card (TJ, 8 Oct 2026)
In the original component the picture has no fade of its own: it is the first stop on the line. The line grows straight out of it as the section holds, and the picture is carried along with the track and pushed off the screen. The card now works the same way, turned vertical. A dot (.vt__cap) sits on the card's lower edge, centred on the axis. As the section holds the line draws down out of it to 7am, then 7.30am and on; once the tip reaches 56 percent of the screen (60 on phones) the track rises and carries the card up and out under the header. Each hour now starts when the tip actually reaches its dot (it was evenly spaced, which drifted once hours had different heights). The card is measured at its resting place, not its lowered entrance start. The scroll in lift and frost stays. Measured at 1440x900 and 390x844: dot on the card's edge to the pixel, card leaves the top as the line passes 7.30am, no errors. Backups: lab/day-timeline.before-card-line.js.bak and .css.bak.

Recommendation 1 of four, built with hero_arrival_v2_swapped (TPS images; now assets/img/hero_arrival_v2_swapped.webp, 2400x1340). One section: the old Experience title card (wx) is hidden (id experience-original, hidden attribute) and kept as a backup.

Then the line grows from the dot on the seam between photograph and card and the hours follow as before, the photograph riding up and off with the track like the component's picture.

The tip of the line holds at 56 percent (60 on phones), never above the head row's foot, capped at 72 percent on short screens with the photograph easing onto the track's offset as it shrinks. ## The Four Kinds of Care: held stage (TJ, 8 Oct 2026)
Adapted from the 21st.dev Services Stack (ivarunchaudhary, reference A in lab/what-we-do-refs.html), code ours, not copied. New files kinds.css and kinds.js; new section .kc (data-kc) replaces the stacked cards, which stay hidden as the backup (data-section four-kinds-of-care-original). Backup of the page: lab/care.before-kinds-stage.html.bak.
Stage: sticky, 1 + 7 units x 0.6 screen heights. Each kind holds one unit; each change takes one: the new glass card slides up from below (power2.out) over the last; the last dissolves back into the room (opacity to 0, scale to .9, drifts up 6 percent of the screen); the ground cross fades; the kind's name on the left rises out through its mask and the next rises in; the Ember tick on the rail moves on. The first card and name rise in as the section scrolls into view. The ground is the active card's own photograph blurred 42px under a 50 percent Canvas veil, so each card dissolves into its own room's colours.
Card: frosted glass (canvas .74), portrait photograph left (4:5), then the promise (Instrument Serif), the paragraph (Karla), the detail sections' own headings as chips, and Read the detail. The card's heading is screen reader only while live (the name on the left titles it). If the tallest card does not fit the screen, .is-tight narrows the portrait column and the reading type (1280x720 uses it).
Placeholder portraits, cropped 4:5 from the site's own images: kc-mother-baby (s2-handoff), kc-treatments (massage), kc-meals (s6-meal), kc-home-support (hero_household-wide_v2_ironing). To be replaced by generated images.
Phones and reduced motion: the four cards as a plain list. Measured at 1920x1080, 1440x900, 1280x720, 390x844: every card fits, no horizontal scroll, no errors. Bug found and fixed on the way: a CSS start offset on the names added to GSAP's own, so each name stopped half under its mask.

## The Four Kinds of Care: one pair of hands (TJ, 8 Oct 2026)
Replaces the card stage (TJ: generic, "a normal slideshow"); the card stage (.kc) is hidden as a backup and kinds.js now skips a hidden section. The four detail sections (mother-baby, treatments, meals, home-support) are hidden; their titles and paragraphs moved verbatim into this section. Your Postpartum Specialist stays outside (TJ). Backup: lab/care.before-hands-stage.html.bak. New files hands.css, hands.js.
Concept: one woman, one home, four kinds of care. Four full screen photographs of the specialist in side profile, her head in the same place in each (measured within 4px at 2528 wide; the laundry image was shifted 4px to match), carrying the baby (putty nursery), the tungku (white bedroom), the soup tray (the care-kitchen kitchen), the folded laundry (sage laundry room). Generated in Codex then edited with Nano Banana 2.1 through WaveSpeed; each room given its own palette so the set does not read as one AI tone (client feedback). Sources in TPS images/kinds-of-care/; site copies assets/img/hands-*.webp (2400x1611).
Stage: sticky, 1 + 7 units x 0.7 screen heights. Each kind holds one unit; each change takes one: the next photograph cross fades in over the last (only the room and what she carries change), the outgoing words rise out through line masks and the incoming rise in, the item list fades across, the Ember tick moves on, any open item closes. The first kind's words rise in as the section arrives. A soft Canvas veil over the left 58 percent carries the words. Name in Instrument Serif capitals, promise in Instrument Serif, paragraph in Karla; detail items are quiet rows with a plus that open their paragraph in place, one at a time. If a kind would not fit, .is-tight shortens the paragraph to three lines (click opens it) and the name; no tested size needed it.
Phones and reduced motion: each kind is its photograph (4:3, framed on her) followed by its words and items. Measured at 1920x1080, 1440x900, 1280x720, 390x844: fits, no horizontal scroll, no errors.

## The Four Kinds of Care: hands only (TJ, 8 Oct 2026)
TJ: the full figure version risks reading as AI (her identical pose in four rooms could not have been shot for real) and could erode trust. Switched to hands only: tight crops of the same four photographs, hands and object on the right, soft room on the left for the words, the pink sleeve with the white piped cuff carrying continuity. Crops (1300x867 from the 2528x1696 sources, hands at 64 percent across) in TPS images/kinds-of-care/hands-crops/, upscaled 4x with wavespeed-ai/image-upscaler (faithful, no regeneration) to 5016x3345, site copies assets/img/hands-crop-*.webp at 2880x1920. Section class hd--hands frames the photographs on the hands (object-position 60% 55%). Alt text rewritten for hands. The full figure images (hands-*.webp) stay in assets as the backup; lab/care.before-hands-crop-test.html.bak is the page before the switch.
Known: the straight cross fade ghosts two pairs of hands for a moment; a fade through the room colour or a wipe may suit hands better.

## The Four Kinds of Care: lift in, lower out (TJ, 8 Oct 2026)
The dissolve had lost its reason once the full figure went, so each kind is now two layers: the room with her removed (plate) and the hands with what they hold, cut out (TJ made both sets in TPS images/kinds-of-care/hands-crops/cutouts/, 5016x3345; site copies assets/img/hands-plate-*.webp and hands-cut-*.webp at 2880x1920, the cut outs with alpha). Both layers are the same crop, so they share one box and one object position and line up exactly (measured 0px offset at rest).
Motion (hands.js): the first pair of hands lifts into the room as the section arrives (from 35 percent of the screen below, with the first words). At each change the hands are lowered out of the bottom of the frame (power2.in, first half of the move, as if setting the thing down), the room cross fades behind while it is empty, and the next pair is lifted in from below (power2.out, second half). The words change as before. A soft drop shadow sits under the hands so they read as in the room. Phones and reduced motion: each scene is the plate with the cut out over it, one still picture. The single image version stays in assets (hands-crop-*.webp) and lab/care.before-hands-lift.html.bak is the page before the switch.
Measured at 1440x900, 1280x720, 390x844: layers aligned, sequence correct (hands out at 21 percent, plate half faded, next hands landing by 33), no horizontal scroll, no errors.

## The Four Kinds of Care: layered sideways motion (TJ, 8 Oct 2026)
Replaces the lift in, lower out (TJ: lowering the hands showed a headless torso; nothing vertical, the front image must never show its top or bottom edge, the hands should only move just out of position and slide back). Three layers at three speeds, sideways only, all scrubbed by scroll: the words still, the room slow, the hands faster.
Scale first, then move: rooms at 1.08 (4 percent spare each side), travel +2 to -2 percent and zoom to 1.10 over the whole time each room is on screen, including its changes, so motion carries through. Hands at 1.12 (6 percent spare), drifting 4 points right to left while their kind holds. At a change nothing leaves the frame: the outgoing hands slide 3.5 points further left and fade (first half), the room cross fades behind, the incoming hands fade in from 4 points right and 1.15 scale, settling into place (second part, power2.out). Per kind extra shift right (0, 3, 8, 10 points for baby, tungku, tray, laundry) keeps every object clear of the words; the text column narrowed to min(36vw, 520px). Moving the hands right only exposes the cut out's transparent side; only leftward travel is bounded.
Measured at 41 scroll points at 1920x1080, 1440x900 and 1280x720: no visible layer ever exposes an edge inside the stage, no errors. Backup of the previous script: lab/hands.before-parallax.js.bak.

## One timeline language, Tailored rebuilt, the circle becomes the sun (TJ, 9 Oct 2026)
TJ's brief: one universal language across every line that progresses, taken from A Day With Us (the 21st.dev Product Timeline adaptation), and a circle at the point of motion everywhere. The circle at the end of Tailored's line lifts off and becomes the sun in Day and Night. Restore point before this work: commit 1a99e96.

The language (site.css :root): --tline 1px, --tline-track, --tline-ink (Ember Deep on light grounds, white on dark), --tdot 11px for each stop, --ttip 15px for the circle at the point of motion, --tease. A Day With Us now reads them and gained the travelling circle at the tip of its line (.vt__tip); otherwise unchanged.

Tailored For You, rebuilt (what-we-do-tailored.css and .js; the old .tail styles and tailoredMotion() are removed). One photograph (hero_night-shift_v1); the kitchen, living room and sofa pictures and the retired room logic are gone. Ground: the footer's Ink Mute with the blush, rose mist and haze drift (TJ: never comfortable with the clay), faded out at the foot so it meets the grey gap below with no seam. Desktop: a held stage (420vh); the photograph opens full screen under "Tailored To You" (Quentin) and closes onto a 4:3 slot on the left while the glass card lifts in on the right with the same Quentin line in Ember Deep, the lead and the copy (Read more scrolls inside the card). Then the white line draws 0 to 5 days and travels 5, 7, 14, 30, resting at each, while the three figures count; the ledger in what-we-do.js is unchanged apart from reading a vertical rail and letting this script drive it (data-ty-driven). Clicking, keying or dragging to a day scrolls the page to it. Phones: the photograph closes onto its frame as it scrolls in, Quentin title over it; the line runs down the centre with the days alternating either side, its circle held at 72 percent of the screen, the figures three across above it so they stay in view. Positions are read live every frame because the zones above size themselves after load and any stored position drifts (measured 50px).

The sun: a twin of the circle (.ty-sun, fixed) lifts off the end of the line once the line is complete and the section leaves, and flies to the Day and Night sun, growing from 15px to the sun's size, landing at the moment the clock begins (8 percent of a screen into the pin), where the real sun takes over. Across and down on desktop, straight down the centre on phones (TJ's correction: the centred vertical line marries up with the centred sun). The real circle and the real sun are hidden while it flies, so there is only one disc. The gap above the photograph now takes Tailored's grey with no tint (data-ground-sink="0" read by what-we-do-promise.js).

Housekeeping zones, room list only (TJ: everything else stays). Desktop: the hairline is the track; the line fills down to the room in view with the circle at its tip, and each room keeps a stop once reached; at the whole house lit, the line runs to the end. Sizing raised to suit the circle (.76rem, 17px rows, 30px indent). Phones: the tab row gets the same line running across, filled to the chosen room with the circle on it. The zones lock (index/what-we-do.html:housekeeping-zones) was already unlocked; its baseline is stale until TJ approves and it is recorded again.

Noted, not changed: the client's kitchen copy reads "After l meals" (also in research/what-we-do/copy.json).
Checked at 1440x900 and 390x844 through every phase, and with reduced motion: no errors. Backups: lab/*.before-timeline-language.*.bak.

## Tailored For You: the card and photograph swap, Read more grows the glass (TJ, 9 Oct 2026)
TJ disliked the scrollbar that appeared inside the card on Read more. The people in the photograph are all on its right (mother centre right, specialist and crib far right), so the card is now on the left and the photograph on the right: the glass can grow into the empty wall and bedding without covering anyone.
Desktop: the photograph closes onto the right-hand slot. Read more widens the card rightward across the photograph (a negative right margin driven by --ty-g, a registered @property that transitions 0 to 1 over 800ms). The frost is its own layer (.ty__card::before) with a mask that fades its right 34 percent out toward the photograph, so the two read as one surface; the words keep to the solid part (children max-width 100 percent minus 34 percent of the open card). No scrollbar anywhere in the card: open, the copy reflows into the wider glass and is set slightly closer (.76 to .88rem, tighter gaps; .74rem on short screens). Verified no overflow at 1440x900, 1280x720 and 1920x1080 (the card fits with 0px spare to scroll). Read less reverses it. The track now sits above the photograph layer (z 4) so the glass can pass over the picture; its parts are invisible until the photograph has closed. Phones are unchanged: the photograph above, the card full width growing taller on Read more.
Backups: lab/*.before-card-swap.*.bak.
- Smoother blend (TJ, 9 Oct 2026: the first fade was too harsh). Two causes, both fixed. The glass's fade was a straight linear ramp with a visible start; it is now nine eased stops on a smoothstep across the right 46 percent. And the photograph's own left edge showed through the frost as a hard step of picture against slate; it now fades in as well, with a matching eased mask on the photograph driven by the same --ty-g (moved up to the section so card and photograph share it) and by the slot's left edge and width (--ph-l, --ty-sw). Closed, the photograph is untouched. No overflow at 1440x900, 1280x720, 1920x1080.

## The sun is born from the 14 day circle, dropping straight down (TJ, 9 Oct 2026)
TJ disliked the circle travelling from the 30 stop across to the sun. The line still scrolls to 30, with the circle ending there, but when the section leaves, the circle at the 14 stop lifts off, grows and drops straight down the page's centre into the sun. For that the 14 now sits at exactly the middle of the line (--pos in what-we-do-tailored.css: 5 at 1/6 and 7 at 7/30 as by count, 14 at 1/2, 30 at the end, a small stretch between 7 and 14 that nobody can read as a ruler); measured at 1440 wide, the 14 stop's centre is at x 720 and so is the sun. The dragging in what-we-do.js turns the pointer's place on the line back into days piece by piece. The 14 stop hides while its twin flies, so there is only ever one disc. Phones are unchanged: the line is vertical and centred, and its foot circle drops down the same axis.
Checked: clicking 30, 14, 7 and 5 lands on each; dragging the circle to the 14 lands on 14; no errors at 1440x900 and 1280x720 or on phones. Backups: lab/*.before-14-drop.*.bak.

## Day and Night Care: a Quentin title, simpler labels, the note waits for the dark (TJ, 9 Oct 2026)
Title: "Day and Night Care" in Quentin, twice, as Tailored's is: a white copy under the photograph (on the grey gap) and an ink copy inside it, so the rising wipe turns each letter from white to dark exactly where it passes. It starts top left, sized to end short of the centre line the circle falls down (4.4vw; on phones two short lines, "Day and" over "Night Care"), and glides to the centre once the falling circle's top has cleared its foot, over a fifth of a screen (what-we-do-dn-title.js, read live every frame; it glides back on the way up). The ink copy then eases to cream on the header's own curve (--dnt-c), stays through the sunset and the night, and fades out as the frosted glass arrives (opacity follows --dn-g). Reduced motion: centred from the start. The visible copies are aria-hidden; the section's heading is still the existing .dn-sr h2.
Sunset labels: Morning, Afternoon, Evening, Night (were Every morning and so on).
The small title and paragraph ("Care arranged around the hours you need most"): moved out of the day column into .dnf__note at the bottom right, and they lift in only once night has fully fallen (NIGHT_FULL, over a third of a screen), not as the photograph lands. They still give way as the glass arrives.
Checked through the whole journey at 1440x900 and on phones: no errors. Backups: lab/*.before-dn-title.*.bak.
- Day and Night title size (TJ, 9 Oct 2026): it shrinks to half its size over the same glide, from the moment it starts moving to where it settles at the centre (scaled about its own top centre, --dn-ts), on desktop and phones.

## Day and Night: one colour for everything on the frame (TJ, 9 Oct 2026)
TJ found the colours on the frame did not agree (white sun, line and labels over the bright afternoon, a dark title, then cream at night on separate timings). Now every element follows one story: white on the grey, dark on the afternoon photograph, cream at night. The sun, its horizon line and Morning, Afternoon, Evening, Night take --dnt-c, the variable that already drives the title and runs on the header menu's own curve, so they darken and turn cream in lockstep with the title and the menu. The falling circle from Tailored gets a dark second disc clipped every frame to where the photograph's wipe has risen behind it (the frame's top plus its clip-path inset), in the live --dnt-c, so the circle turns dark exactly where the photograph's edge passes and lands already the sun's colour. Checked through the crossing (half white, half dark at the edge) and the sunset, at 1440x900 and on phones: no errors. Backups: lab/*.before-one-colour.*.bak.

## Day and Night: the sun is the clock (TJ, 9 Oct 2026)
The sun was setting before the room did: it dipped under the line at 1.42 screens into the pin, night finished at 2.12, and the change to cream was only half done when the sun touched the line. Now night and the change to cream are computed from the sun's own position, not from scroll (what-we-do-promise.js): night has fully fallen when the sun's lower edge touches the horizon line; the change to cream (sun, line, labels, title and the header menu, one value, creamNow and --dnt-c) runs from half way to nine tenths of the way there; the dip under the line is the last beat, with Night lit. Then the clock clears and the small title and paragraph lift in. The descent is longer (PEAK_PIN_VH 1.92 to 2.4) and the beat held on full night after it shorter (DN_BEAT_VH 1.3 to 0.82), so the section is the same length and the Tailored circle's handoff is unmoved. Measured at 1440x900: cream complete at 1.0 screens into the pin, night full at 1.1, sun touches at 1.2 (already cream), fully under at 1.8, paragraph in by 2.1. Phones: touches at 1.3, already cream. No errors. Backup: lab/what-we-do-promise.before-sun-clock.js.bak.
- Day and Night title resting size (TJ, 9 Oct 2026): 20 percent larger than half, so 0.6 of its starting size once settled in the centre; the starting size is unchanged.
- Day and Night small title (TJ, 9 Oct 2026): "Care arranged around the hours you need most" is now in the paragraph's own font (Karla) in capitals, instead of Instrument Serif, at a size and spacing that sit with the text below it (about .9 to 1.1rem, .06em tracking).
- Day and Night small title, as a title (TJ, 9 Oct 2026): it read too close to the paragraph. Now Karla 700 capitals, a quarter larger (about 1.12 to 1.4rem), .08em tracking, a little more space beneath. Italic was considered and set aside: italic capitals in Karla read as a quote, and italics already mark the "Ideal if" lines on this frame.
- Day and Night small title (TJ, 9 Oct 2026): not bold (back to Karla 400, still a quarter larger and open tracked), and "you need most" on its own line (a line break in the markup). On phones it is set a touch smaller (min 1.12rem or 4.3vw) so "Care arranged around the hours" holds one line.

## The mothers in our photographs (TJ, 9 Oct 2026)
The woman receiving care changes from image to image: Black, blonde, brunette and more, so every client can see herself. Each one looks like a mother who has just had a baby, not a model: often a softer, fuller figure, little or no makeup, natural skin, a natural look that is real without being unflattering. The specialist stays the same person (the pink uniform specialist) across the site.

## Day and Night Care on phones: a shorter swipe, a Day to Night line to tap, no falling ball (TJ, 12 Oct 2026)
TJ: on phones, no ball dropping from Tailored (desktop keeps it); one horizontal tappable line, Day and Night (TJ asked for morning and night or day and night; Claude proposed Day and Night to match the two options, TJ agreed); the change from day to night a gentle diffusion, not a switch; and, after first leaning to taps only, TJ kept the swipe (something is lost without it) with a shorter scroll, plus taps. "Day and Night Care" should start small and end big.
- what-we-do-tailored.js: fly() does nothing on phones; the 14 stays on its line.
- what-we-do-promise.js, phones (DN_PHONE): the held scrub is 1.8 screens (was 3.1): sunset 0.9 (was 1.5), beat .2 (.35), glass .5 (.8), hold .2 (.45); the order of beats is unchanged. The sun and the four times of day are replaced by .dnl, built in script: Day at the left end, Night at the right, the site's line, fill, end stops and travelling circle (--dnl-t = the sun's old travel, 0 to 1). With no sun, night is complete exactly at the end of the travel (touch = 1), so the circle reaches Night as night has fully fallen. The line stays on screen through the note and fades as the glass builds (so Day is still a tap away at night), taking taps whenever it is visible. dnLineTaps(): a tap on Day or Night scrolls the page, over 2.5s with an ease at both ends, to that end's point in the pin (Day just after the line arrives, Night where night has fully fallen), so scroll stays the one driver and the change reads as a fade; any touch, wheel or key cancels it. The steps use behavior 'instant': site.css sets html { scroll-behavior: smooth }, which restarted every frame and stalled the fade until its last step.
- what-we-do-dn-title.js, phones: centred from the start (measured every frame), half its size, growing to its full size (the doubled clamp) with the night (--dn-nv), then leaving with the glass as before.
- what-we-do-day-night.css, phones: the sundial and states hidden; .dnl styles (labels 13px, 44px tap targets, line and circle in the frame's own --dnt-c colour, dark by day, cream by night); the clock above the answer layer (z 3) so its ends can be tapped.
Measured at 390x844 and 360x740: the section is 2363px tall at 390 (was 3460); walking down, the line, night and the title track together (line .24, night .15, title .53 of full; then .66, .73, .91; then 1, 1, 1); tap Night: line .07 at .6s, .57 at 1.3s, .98 at 2s, 1 by 3.2s, night following; tap Day returns to the start; the title centred at 196 of 390 and 180 of 360; no ball, no stop lifted; no overflow, no errors. - what-we-do-dn-title.js, phones: centred, scale 1 throughout (59px at 390); the two copies still turn it from white to dark as the photograph's edge crosses it.
- what-we-do-promise.js and what-we-do-day-night.css, phones: a sun (30px, in the frame's colour --dnt-c, so it eases from dark to cream on the same curve as the title and the header's menu) sits over the line's middle in its own sky box whose foot is the line, so the line cuts it as it sets. It arrives and leaves with the line, and is driven by the same travel as the circle (--dnl-t): fully up at Day, fully set at Night (as night has fully fallen), rising again on the way back to Day, by swipe or by tap.
Checked at 390x844: walking and tapping as before (line .07, .58, .97, 1 at .6, 1.3, 2, 3.2s after a Night tap; Day returns), the title 297px wide at every point, centred at 196; the sun up and dark at Day, half set and mid tone on the way, gone at Night; no errors, no overflow. Desktop code paths untouched (phone only branches and media queries); the full desktop guard was not rerun for this change. Backups: lab/*.before-dn-sun.*.bak.

## Day and Night Care on phones: the sun starts higher and is larger (TJ, 12 Oct 2026)
TJ: the sun did not start high enough; it should start half way between the "Day and Night Care" title and the line, so it travels much further; and it should be 15 percent larger. what-we-do-promise.js measures the title's foot and the line live each frame (both sit in the pinned frame) and sets the sun's sky box (--dnl-sky) to reach from the line up to that half way point plus half the sun; what-we-do-day-night.css: the sun is 34.5px (was 30), starts at the top of its sky and travels the whole sky plus 2px, so it is fully set into the line at Night. Measured: at 390x844 the sun's centre starts at 392 against a half way point of 389 and sets 160px to the line at 549, nothing showing at Night; at 360x740 it starts at 353 against 349 and sets to 480. No errors. Tool: lab/dn-sun-check.mjs. Backups: lab/*.before-dn-sun-high.*.bak.

## A Day With Us on phones: settled photograph, merged glass, a next dot to tap (TJ, 12 Oct 2026)
TJ: the photograph opening and shrinking into place felt uneasy; have it settled in its final place from the start, closer to the top of the section, like Tailored For You; the glass merged into the image by default; and the line from 7am on drawn by scroll as now and also by tap: the next point a greyed out dot that can be tapped. Text only appears once its hour is reached (as before). Phones only (.is-flow); desktop untouched.
- what-we-do-timeline.css: the photograph is in the page (6:7, 342px wide at 390, 24px gutters, top corners rounded, foot open), 24px under the section above, framed on both figures (object-position 49%). The glass card is merged into its foot by default: it rises 120px over the photograph and its frost fades upward over 130px on the eased nine stops, rounded at the bottom only (Tailored's open state, always on). The travelling "A Day With Us" copies and the veil are hidden; the Quentin line simply sits in the card in coral. The card is there from the start (no lift, no rise).
- what-we-do-timeline.js (phone branch): no photograph journey. The line's tip is held at half the screen (was 62 percent) so the next dot is always in view below it. A button (.vt__go, labelled "Show 7am, A gentle start" and so on) sits over each hour's dot; only the next unreached hour's shows, as a greyed dot (38 percent) with a 44px target, the faint grey axis already running under it. A tap on it carries the page (1.1s, eased both ends, instant steps because html has scroll-behavior smooth) to just past that hour's reach, so the scrubbed line draws to it, its dot grows over the ghost and its words rise, and the next ghost appears; the target is capped just before the next hour so a tap never reveals two. Any touch, wheel or key cancels the glide; swiping is unchanged.
Checked at 390x844 and 360x640: the photograph 342 (312) wide at 24px from the left, the card overlapping it by 120px; four taps in a row revealed hours 1, 2, 3, 4 one at a time (next dot 475 to 521px on screen at 390, 360 to 411 at 360x640), and tapping on to the end showed all 11 hours with no next dot left; no overflow, no errors. Desktop guard (80 shots at 1440): differences under 0.15 percent. Tool: lab/vt-tap-check.mjs, lab/vt-phone-now.mjs. Backups: lab/what-we-do-timeline.before-phone-settled.*.bak.

## Tailored For You on desktop: short windows fit, or the section scrolls (TJ, 12 Oct 2026)
TJ saw the open Read more text spill out of the card and over the DAYS line in a browser window about 605px tall (the IDE below it). Cause: the held desktop stage fits the title, card, line and figures on one screen, so the card's size comes from the window's height (587 by 209px at 1835x605) and the open text needed 256px. Also found: a cliff at 760px tall, where the short laptop rule stopped and the cards dropped to 249px of room, so 761 to 860px tall (1366x768 among them) was already broken. TJ chose Claude's plan, no scrollbar inside the card ever:
- what-we-do-tailored.js desktop(): before drawing, cardFits() measures the card with Read more closed and open (.ty-measure: the open state with transitions off, put back before they return): the words' own extent plus 14px each side must fit the card (they may run into its padding, as the approved 1440x900 layout does). If not, .is-tight (a little closer: smaller lead and paragraphs, tighter padding and gaps); if still not, or the window is under 540px tall, .is-calm: the section stops holding still and scrolls with the page in its finished desktop layout (photograph in its slot beside the card, the title on its line, the line across), the card growing with its words (a ResizeObserver re-places the photograph when the head row changes), the line tapped and dragged through the ledger as on phones, resting on 5 days, all four stops shown (reached solid, the rest faint) so the 14 can still drop into the sun. The geometry was moved into geom() so it can be measured again. Desktop rebuilds on a height change of 40px or more as well as on width.
- what-we-do-tailored.css: the short laptop rule now runs to 860px tall (was 760px); .ty-measure, .is-tight and .is-calm rules (min-width 861px).
Measured at 12 window sizes: 1440x900 held (unchanged), 1440x800 held, 1440x760 held, 1366x768 held, 1440x700 tight, 1536x864 tight, 1280x720 tight, 1835x605 calm, 1280x640 calm, 1920x520 calm, 960x540 calm (200 percent zoom of 1920x1080), 1920x1080 held. At every size the words stay inside the card, closed and open, and Read more is never covered. A step remains at 860/861px tall (the roomier spacing above it makes the cards smaller: 518 to 425px wide); both sides fit. Desktop guard at 1440x900: under 0.1 percent. Tools: lab/ty-fit-check.mjs, lab/ty-fit-probe.mjs, lab/ty-cliff.mjs, lab/ty-short-window.mjs. Backups: lab/what-we-do-tailored.before-desktop-fit.*.bak.
Not yet checked at short heights: the other held desktop sections (hero, Four Kinds of Care, Day and Night, A Day With Us, the house).

## Bespoke Programmes restyle plan written (TJ, 12 Oct 2026)
TJ: the functionality is finished, but the styling copies the example experience rather than What We Do's settled language; the bottom bar should be the What We Do progression line, the Back, Begin and Next buttons are ugly, the small BESPOKE PROGRAMMES label must go (the client has spoken against that styling; Quentin instead), and every card (the tinted note, the 01 to 03 cards, the choice cards) should give way to text on the ground, glass where possible, and selections as radio, checkbox or field controls rather than cards. Claude's strategy, approved as a plan for smaller models to execute: BESPOKE-RESTYLE-PLAN.md (project root), eight chats, each built from one of What We Do's five devices (the progression line, Quentin over capitals, glass merged into a photograph, hairline rows with a filling dot, the underlined spaced capitals link). Nothing built yet.

## Bespoke restyle, chat 1: the frame (TJ, 12 Oct 2026)
Built from BESPOKE-RESTYLE-PLAN.md chat 1 on build-your-programme.html. The deck, the pricing engine, the rules and the copy are unchanged.
- **The bar is What We Do's progression line (Device A).** The crumbs row ("00 Start", "Step 2 of 7") is gone. The bar now holds the step name (the page's data-title) in Tailored's DAYS style (Montserrat 500, .66rem, .18em, uppercase, opacity .7), the line, and Back and Next at the two ends. The line: hairline track, ember fill (--tline-ink) and the --ttip circle moving on --tease over 650ms, a --tdot stop per page, solid once reached and faint at .45 before. Above each stop its number, 00 to 08, Instrument Serif at clamp(1.1rem, 1.6vw, 1.5rem), .38 at rest and 1 for the current page. Each stop is a 44px button (built by the deck script as before); reached stops and the next one are tappable (the .vt__go idea), stops further on do nothing. The tap goes through the existing go().
- **Back and Next are the Read more link (Device E)**: Montserrat 500, .72rem (phones .8125rem), .16em, uppercase, ink, 1px underline at 5px, 44px tall. Next is the ember .btn--primary only as Begin (start) and Book a call (summary); the deck script swaps the classes by page index. Back is hidden on the start page (visibility, so the line keeps its length) and still disabled there. No .btn--outline remains in the bar.
- **The eyebrow is Quentin (Device B, TJ).** The BESPOKE PROGRAMMES label is replaced by "Bespoke Programmes" in Quentin, coral deep, sized exactly as .op__kicker (desktop clamp(3.6rem, min(7.6vw, 12.4vh), 7rem), phones clamp(2.8rem, min(15vw, 7vh), 4rem)). The page's one Quentin line. Note (Claude): at those sizes it sets on two lines at both 1440 and 390, since "Bespoke Programmes" is much longer than "What we do"; left for TJ to judge with chat 2's start page.
- **The start page hint** ("Swipe, or tap Next, to begin...") is removed.
- **Layout (Claude).** Desktop: four fixed columns (Back 4rem, name 11rem, line, Next 11.5rem) so the line never changes length between pages (452px at 1440). Phones: the name on its own row on the left, the line full width (342px at 390), Back and Next beneath; "What matters most" beside "See my programme" did not fit one row at 390. Bar height: 90px at 1440 (was about 84), 145px at 390 (was about 136).
- **Measured** (lab/bespoke-frame-shots.mjs, all nine pages at 1440x900 and 390x844): the fill's end and the circle's centre sit exactly on the current stop's centre on every page (0.00px off); tapping the reached 02 from page 04 goes to 02, then the next dot 03 goes to 03, and 07 (unreached) stays on 03, at both widths; all stops 44px wide; no sideways overflow; no visible .btn--outline in the bar. Ember buttons: the bar's Next is ember only on the start and summary pages. Two more .btn--primary remain in the page: "Request my consultation" on the summary card (chat 7 turns it into a link) and the unused phone .feebar, which is never shown on the deck page. So the plan's count of 2 is met by the bar and reached in full only after chat 7 (and removing the dead feebar, which nothing in the plan schedules).
- **Checks.** programme-check 49 of 49; deck-check passes (crumb readout changed to the current stop's number and the step name); bespoke-check 15 of 15 UI cases and bespoke-phone run clean. bespoke-check and bespoke-phone already failed before this chat (they still read the Build 10 ticker and panel and scrolled the window, both gone since the deck of 10 Oct); both were ported to the deck. Header and footer sync checks ok on every page.
- **Tools and backups.** lab/bespoke-frame-shots.mjs <outdir> [full] (screens plus the measurements above). Before: lab/bespoke-before-restyle/ (full page, nine pages, both widths). After: lab/bespoke-restyle-1/. Backups: lab/build-your-programme.before-restyle-1.{html,css,js}.bak, lab/deck-check.before-restyle-1.mjs.bak, lab/bespoke-check.before-restyle-1.mjs.bak, lab/bespoke-phone.before-restyle-1.mjs.bak.
